(b) Conduct of Business by Parent. Except for matters set forth in Section 5.1(b) of the Parent Disclosure Letter, as required by Applicable Law, as contemplated by this Agreement, or as otherwise consented to by the Company in writing (such consent not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed), during the period from the date of this Agreement to the Effective Time, Parent (x) shall, and shall cause its subsidiaries to, use reasonable best efforts to (1) carry on their respective businesses in all material respects in the ordinary course, (2) preserve intact its material assets, properties, Parent Material Contracts and business organizations, (3) keep available the services of its current officers and key employees, and (4) preserve its current relationships with material customers, suppliers, distributors, lessors, licensors, licensees, creditors, contractors and other persons with whom Parent or any of its subsidiaries has material business relations and (y) shall not, and shall cause its subsidiaries not to:

(i) (A) other than (x) Parent’s regular quarterly cash dividends as set forth in Section 5.1(b)(i) of the Parent Disclosure Letter that are consistent with Parent’s past practice, including with respect to record and payment dates for such dividends, and (y) dividends and distributions by a direct or indirect wholly owned subsidiary of Parent to Parent or another wholly owned subsidiary of Parent, declare, set aside or pay any dividends on, make any other distributions in respect of any of its capital stock or other equity interests or (B) split, combine, recapitalize, or reclassify any of its capital stock or other equity interests or securities or issue or authorize the issuance of any other securities in respect of, in lieu of or in substitution for shares of its capital stock or other equity interests;

(ii) issue, deliver, sell, pledge or otherwise encumber or subject to any Lien (other than Permitted Liens) any shares of its capital stock or any other equity interests or securities, including any securities convertible into, or any rights, warrants or options to acquire, any such shares, equity interests or securities, (other than (A) Parent Equity Awards issued in the ordinary course of business, (B) the issuance of shares of Parent Common Stock upon the exercise, vesting or settlement of Parent Equity Awards, outstanding as of the date of this Agreement in accordance with their present terms or granted after the date of this Agreement to the extent permitted by this Section 5.1(a)(ii), (C) upon the conversion of the Parent Series A Preferred Stock outstanding as of the date of this Agreement in accordance with their terms in effect on the date of this Agreement; (D) as required by any Parent Benefit Plan in effect on the date of this Agreement or entered into or amended in accordance with the terms of this Agreement, (E) issuances of Parent Common Stock in connection with the exercise of Parent Warrants that are outstanding as of the date hereof in accordance with the terms thereof,

 

-51-

(F) issuances of Parent Common Stock as contingent consideration in an acquisition transaction based on arrangements in effect as of the date hereof and disclosed in the Parent SEC Documents and (G) solely with respect to the capital stock, equity interests or other securities of Parent’s direct or indirect wholly owned subsidiaries, transactions solely between Parent and such wholly owned subsidiaries, or among Parent’s wholly owned subsidiaries);

(iii) change any of its material financial accounting policies or procedures currently in effect, except (A) as required by GAAP, Regulation S-X of the Exchange Act or a Governmental Entity or quasi-governmental authority (including the Financial Accounting Standards Board or any similar organization) or (B) as required by Applicable Law;

(iv) (A) amend the Certificate of Incorporation of Parent (other than the Parent Charter Amendment and the Parent Series B Certificate of Designation) or Bylaws of Parent or (B) adopt or implement any plan of complete or partial liquidation, dissolution, consolidation, restructuring, recapitalization or other reorganization (other than transactions solely among Parent’s wholly owned direct and indirect subsidiaries); or

(v) authorize, or commit or agree to take, any of the foregoing actions.

(c) No Right to Control or Direct Operations. Nothing contained in this Agreement is intended to give Parent or the Merger Subs, directly or indirectly, the right to control or direct the operations of the Company or its subsidiaries prior to the Effective Time, and nothing contained in this Agreement is intended to give the Company, directly or indirectly, the right to control or direct the operations of Parent or its subsidiaries prior to the Effective Time, in each case, in violation of Applicable Law. Prior to the Effective Time, each of Parent, the Merger Subs and the Company shall exercise, consistent with the terms and conditions of this Agreement, complete control and supervision over its and its subsidiaries’ operations.

(d) Process Related to Affirmative Covenants and Forbearance Covenants.

(i) If the Company or any of its subsidiaries desire to take an action that would be prohibited by Section 5.1(a) without the prior approval of Parent, then, prior to taking such action, the Company and its subsidiaries (in lieu of the procedure outlined in Section 9.2) may request consent by sending an email to each of the individuals listed in Section 5.1(d)(i) of the Parent Disclosure Letter specifying, in reasonable detail, the action proposed to be taken (or omitted from being taken). Any of the individuals listed in Section 5.1(d)(i) of the Parent Disclosure Letter may grant consent on behalf of Parent by email.

(ii) If Parent or any of its subsidiaries desire to take an action that would be prohibited by Section 5.1(b) without the prior approval of the Company, then, prior to taking such action, Parent and its subsidiaries (in lieu of the procedure outlined in Section 9.2) may request consent by sending an email to each of the individuals listed in Section 5.1(d)(ii) of the Company Disclosure Letter specifying, in reasonable detail, the action proposed to be taken (or omitted from being taken). Any of the individuals listed in Section 5.1(d)(ii) of the Company Disclosure Letter may grant consent on behalf of the Company by email.

 

-52-

Section 5.2. No Solicitation by the Company.

(a) Immediately following the execution of this Agreement, the Company shall cease and terminate, shall cause its controlled affiliates, and its and their directors and officers to cease and terminate, and shall use reasonable best efforts to cause its other Representatives to cease and terminate, any existing solicitation of, or discussions or negotiations with, any Company Third Party relating to any Company Alternative Transaction. Except as otherwise expressly permitted pursuant to this Section 5.2, the Company shall not, and shall cause its controlled affiliates and its and their officers and directors, not to, and shall use reasonable best efforts to cause its other Representatives not to, directly or indirectly, (i) solicit, initiate or knowingly encourage, or take any other action to knowingly facilitate the submission of any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that is or would reasonably be expected to result in a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal (except to notify a Company Third Party of the existence of this Section 5.2(a)), (ii) participate in any discussions or negotiations with, furnish any nonpublic information relating to the Company or any of its subsidiaries, or afford access to the business, properties, assets, books, records or Representatives of the Company or any of its subsidiaries to, or otherwise knowingly cooperate with, any Company Third Party, with respect to any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that is or would reasonably be expected to result in a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, or (iii) enter into any letter of intent, memorandum of understanding, merger agreement, acquisition agreement or other Contract relating to a Company Alternative Transaction; provided that, if, at any time prior to obtaining the Company Stockholder Approval, the Board of Directors of the Company determines in good faith (after consultation with its outside counsel and financial advisors) that a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal made after the date of this Agreement that did not result from a material breach of this Section 5.2(a) constitutes or would reasonably be expected to result in a Company Superior Proposal, subject to compliance with Section 5.2(d) (including prior written notice in accordance with the terms thereof) and pursuant to a Company Acceptable Confidentiality Agreement, the Company and its Representatives may (A) furnish information with respect to the Company and its subsidiaries to the Company Third Party making such proposal (and its Representatives) (provided that all such information has previously been provided to Parent or is provided to Parent prior to or concurrently with the time it is provided to such person) and (B) participate in discussions or negotiations regarding such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal with the Company Third Party making such proposal and its Representatives. Within forty-eight (48) hours after the execution of this Agreement, the Company shall (1) request in writing that each person that has heretofore executed a confidentiality agreement in connection with its consideration of a potential Company Alternative Transaction within twelve (12) months prior to the execution of this Agreement promptly destroy or return to the Company all information heretofore furnished by the Company or any of its Representatives to such person or any of its Representatives in connection therewith and (2) terminate access to any physical or electronic data rooms with respect to a potential Company Alternative Transaction by such person and its Representatives.

(b) Except as permitted by Section 5.2(c) or Section 5.2(e), neither the Board of Directors of the Company nor any committee thereof shall (i) withdraw, qualify or modify, or propose publicly to withdraw, qualify or modify, or fail to make, in each case in a manner adverse to Parent, the Company Board Recommendation, (ii) endorse, approve or recommend, or propose publicly to endorse, approve or recommend, any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal or any Company Alternative Transaction, (iii) fail to include in the Joint Proxy Statement the Company Board Recommendation, (iv) fail to recommend against acceptance of any tender offer or exchange offer for the Company Common Stock, within ten (10) business days after the commencement of such offer (or, if earlier, at least two (2) business days prior to the Company Stockholders Meeting); or (v) fail to publicly reaffirm the Company Board Recommendation within ten (10) business days of Parent’s written request to do so (or, if earlier, at least two (2) business days prior to the Company Stockholders Meeting) following the public announcement of any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal (or any material amendment of any such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, including any change to the price or form of consideration) (provided that if such announcement is made fewer than three (3) business days prior to such meeting, then, notwithstanding the foregoing, the Board of Directors of the Company shall have at least one (1) business day to make such reaffirmation); provided, that the Board of Directors of the Company shall not be required to make such reaffirmation more than one time with respect to any particular Company Alternative Transaction or with respect to any material amendment thereto, including any change to the price or form of consideration (any action or failure to act in clauses (i) through (v) being referred to as a “Company Recommendation Change”).

 

-53-

(c) Notwithstanding the foregoing, in the event that, prior to obtaining the Company Stockholder Approval, the Board of Directors of the Company determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal made after the date of this Agreement that did not arise from a material breach of Section 5.2(a), is a Company Superior Proposal, the Board of Directors of the Company may (i) effect a Company Recommendation Change, or (ii) authorize the Company to terminate this Agreement pursuant to Section 8.1(h) to enter into a Specified Company Agreement with respect to such Company Superior Proposal, in each case, if, and only if, (A) the Board of Directors of the Company determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that the failure to take such action would be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law, (B) the Company has (1) provided prior written notice to Parent to the effect that the Board of Directors of the Company has received a written Company Alternative Transaction Proposal that has not been withdrawn; (2) concluded in good faith (after consultation with its financial advisor and outside legal counsel) that such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal constitutes a Company Superior Proposal; (3) resolved to effect a Company Recommendation Change or to terminate this Agreement pursuant to Section 8.1(h); and (4) provided Parent with a copy of the proposed definitive agreements and other proposed transaction documentation with respect to such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal (including copies of any agreements, commitments or instruments with respect to the financing thereof), and the identity of the person making such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, (C) the Company has complied in all material respects with Section 5.2(d) with respect to such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, (D) for a period of four (4) business days following the notice delivered pursuant to clause (B) of this Section 5.2(c), the Company shall have discussed and negotiated in good faith, and made the Company’s Representatives available to discuss and negotiate in good faith (in each case to the extent Parent desires to negotiate) with Parent’s Representatives, any proposed modifications to the terms and conditions of this Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement so that the Company Alternative Transaction Proposal is no longer a Company Superior Proposal; provided that any amendment to any material term or condition of any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal shall require a new notice and a new negotiation period that shall expire on the later to occur of (I) three (3) business days following delivery of such new notice from the Company to Parent, and (II) the expiration of the original four (4) business day period, and (E) following the end of such negotiation period, the Board of Directors of the Company shall have determined in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, and after considering the terms of any proposed amendment or modification to this Agreement by Parent, that (x) the Company Alternative Transaction Proposal that is the subject of the notice described in clause (B) above still constitutes a Company Superior Proposal, and (y) the failure to take such action would still be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law.

(d) In addition to the obligations of the Company set forth in Section 5.2(a) and Section 5.2(c), the Company shall promptly, and in any event within twenty-four (24) hours of receipt thereof, advise Parent in writing of any Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that would reasonably be expected to result in a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal, the identity of the person making such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer, the material terms thereof and copies of all portions documents and substantive written communications received by the Company or any of its Representatives with respect thereto, in each case that contain terms and conditions thereto (or, as applicable, a summary of any substantive verbal communications received by the Company or any of its Representatives with respect thereto). The Company shall (i) keep Parent reasonably informed of

 

-54-

the status and details of any such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer on a reasonably current basis, including any amendments or proposed amendments to the material terms thereof, and (ii) provide to Parent as soon as reasonably practicable after receipt or delivery thereof copies of all substantive written communications that contain terms and conditions thereto and all drafts of definitive documentation exchanged between the Company or its subsidiaries or any of their Representatives, on the one hand, and any person making such Company Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer, on the other hand (or, as applicable, a summary of any substantive verbal communications with respect thereto).

(e) Other than in connection with a Company Alternative Transaction Proposal (which shall be subject to Section 5.2(c)), prior to obtaining the Company Stockholder Approval, the Board of Directors of the Company may, in response to a Company Intervening Event, make a Company Recommendation Change pursuant to clause (i), clause (iii) or clause (v) of the definition thereof, if, and only if, (A) the Board of Directors of the Company determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that the failure to take such action would be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law, (B) the Company has notified Parent in writing that it intends to effect a Company Recommendation Change pursuant to this Section 5.2(e), including the reasons therefor in reasonable detail, (C) for a period of four (4) business days following the notice delivered pursuant to clause (B) of this Section 5.2(e), the Company shall have discussed and negotiated in good faith and made the Company’s Representatives available to discuss and negotiate in good faith (in each case to the extent Parent desires to negotiate) with Parent’s Representatives any proposed modifications to the terms and conditions of this Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement so that the failure to effect a Company Recommendation Change would no longer be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law; provided that any change to the material facts and circumstances relating to such Company Intervening Event shall require a new notice and a new negotiation period that shall expire on the later to occur of (I) three (3) business days following delivery of such new notice from the Company to Parent and (II) the expiration of the original four (4) business day period, and (D) following the end of such negotiation period, the Board of Directors of the Company shall have determined in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, and after considering the terms of any proposed amendment or modification to this Agreement by Parent, that the failure to effect a Company Recommendation Change would still be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law.

(f) Nothing contained in this Section 5.2 shall prohibit or restrict the Company from (i) taking and disclosing to its stockholders a position contemplated by Rule 14d-9 or Rule 14e-2(a) promulgated under the Exchange Act, or from issuing a “stop, look and listen” statement or similar communication of the type contemplated by Rule 14d-9(f) under the Exchange Act pending disclosure of its position thereunder and (ii) making any required disclosure to the stockholders of the Company if the Board of Directors of the Company determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside counsel, that the failure to take such action would be reasonably likely to be inconsistent with Applicable Law; provided, that the Company expressly reaffirms the Company Board Recommendation in any such public disclosure, other than such a “stop, look and listen” statement or similar communication; provided, further, that the Company shall not effect a Company Recommendation Change other than pursuant to Section 5.2(c) or Section 5.2(e).

(g) Any action by (i) any controlled affiliate of the Company or any director or officer of the Company or any of its controlled affiliates or any of the Company Financial Advisors, or (ii) any other Representative of the Company or any of its subsidiaries to the extent the Company authorizes, or knowingly permits such action that, in each case, if taken by the Company would be a breach of this Section 5.2, shall be deemed to constitute a breach by the Company of this Section 5.2 provided that any action by any Company Financial Advisor shall not constitute a breach of this Section 5.2 by the Company for the purposes of Section 7.2(b), Section 7.2(d) or Section 8.1(c) unless the Company authorizes, or knowingly permits, such action.

 

-55-

Section 5.3. No Solicitation by Parent.

(a) Immediately following the execution of this Agreement, Parent shall cease and terminate, shall cause its controlled affiliates, and its and their directors and officers to cease and terminate, and shall use reasonable best efforts to cause its other Representatives to cease and terminate, any existing solicitation of, or discussions or negotiations with, any Parent Third Party relating to any Parent Alternative Transaction. Except as otherwise expressly permitted pursuant to this Section 5.3, Parent shall not, and shall cause its controlled affiliates and its and their officers and directors not to, and shall use reasonable best efforts to cause its other Representatives not to, directly or indirectly, (i) solicit, initiate or knowingly encourage, or take any other action to knowingly facilitate the submission of any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that is or would reasonably be expected to result in a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal (except to notify a Parent Third Party of the existence of this Section 5.3(a)), (ii) participate in any discussions or negotiations with, furnish any nonpublic information relating to Parent or any of its subsidiaries, or afford access to the business, properties, assets, books, records or Representatives of Parent or any of its subsidiaries to, or otherwise knowingly cooperate with, any Parent Third Party, with respect to any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that is or would reasonably be expected to result in a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, or (iii) enter into any letter of intent, memorandum of understanding, merger agreement, acquisition agreement or other Contract relating to a Parent Alternative Transaction; provided that, if, at any time prior to obtaining the Parent Stockholder Approvals, the Board of Directors of Parent determines in good faith (after consultation with its outside counsel and financial advisors) that a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal made after the date of this Agreement that did not result from a material breach of this Section 5.3(a) constitutes or would reasonably be expected to result in a Parent Superior Proposal, subject to, compliance with Section 5.3(d) (including prior written notice in accordance with the terms thereof) and pursuant to a Parent Acceptable Confidentiality Agreement, Parent and its Representatives may (A) furnish information with respect to Parent and its subsidiaries to the Parent Third Party making such proposal (and its Representatives) (provided that all such information has previously been provided to the Company or is provided to the Company prior to or concurrently with the time it is provided to such person) and (B) participate in discussions or negotiations regarding such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal with the Parent Third Party making such proposal and its Representatives. Within forty-eight (48) hours after the execution of this Agreement, Parent shall (1) request in writing that each person that has heretofore executed a confidentiality agreement in connection with its consideration of a potential Parent Alternative Transaction within twelve (12) months prior to the execution of this Agreement promptly destroy or return to Parent all information heretofore furnished by Parent or any of its Representatives to such person or any of its Representatives in connection therewith and (2) terminate access to any physical or electronic data rooms with respect to a potential Parent Alternative Transaction by such person and its Representatives.

(b) Except as permitted by Section 5.3(c) or Section 5.3(e), neither the Board of Directors of Parent nor any committee thereof shall (i) withdraw, qualify or modify, or propose publicly to withdraw, qualify or modify, or fail to make, in each case in a manner adverse to the Company, the Parent Board Recommendation, (ii) endorse, approve or recommend, or propose publicly to endorse, approve or recommend, any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal or any Parent Alternative Transaction, (iii) fail to include in the Joint Proxy Statement the Parent Board Recommendation, (iv) fail to recommend against acceptance of any tender offer or exchange offer for the Parent Common Stock, within ten (10) business days after the commencement of such offer (or, if earlier, at least two (2) business days prior to the Parent Stockholders Meeting), or (v) fail to publicly reaffirm the Parent Board

 

-56-

Recommendation within ten (10) business days of the Company’s written request to do so (or, if earlier, at least two (2) business days prior to the Parent Stockholders Meeting) following the public announcement of any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal (or any material amendment of any such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, including any change to the price or form of consideration) (provided that if such announcement is made fewer than three (3) business days prior to such meeting, then, notwithstanding the foregoing, the Board of Directors of Parent shall have at least one (1) business day to make such reaffirmation); provided, that the Board of Directors of Parent shall not be required to make such reaffirmation more than one time with respect to any particular Parent Alternative Transaction or with respect to any material amendment thereto, including any change to the price or form of consideration (any action or failure to act in clauses (i) through (v) being referred to as a “Parent Recommendation Change”).

(c) Notwithstanding the foregoing, in the event that, prior to obtaining the Parent Stockholder Approvals, the Board of Directors of Parent determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal made after the date of this Agreement that did not arise from a material breach of Section 5.3(a), is a Parent Superior Proposal, the Board of Directors of Parent may (i) effect a Parent Recommendation Change, or (ii) authorize Parent to terminate this Agreement pursuant to Section 8.1(g) to enter into a Specified Parent Agreement with respect to such Parent Superior Proposal, in each case, if, and only if, (A) the Board of Directors of Parent determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that the failure to take such action would be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law, (B) Parent has (1) provided prior written notice to the Company to the effect that the Board of Directors of Parent has received a written Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal that has not been withdrawn; (2) concluded in good faith (after consultation with its financial advisor and outside legal counsel) that such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal constitutes a Parent Superior Proposal; (3) resolved to effect a Parent Recommendation Change or to terminate this Agreement pursuant to Section 8.1(g); and (4) provided the Company with a copy of the proposed definitive agreements and other proposed transaction documentation with respect to such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal (including copies of any agreements, commitments or instruments with respect to the financing thereof), and the identity of the person making such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, (C) Parent has complied in all material respects with Section 5.3(d) with respect to such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, (D) for a period of four (4) business days following the notice delivered pursuant to clause (B) of this Section 5.3(c), Parent shall have discussed and negotiated in good faith, and made Parent’s Representatives available to discuss and negotiate in good faith (in each case to the extent the Company desires to negotiate) with the Company’s Representatives, any proposed modifications to the terms and conditions of this Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement so that the Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal is no longer a Parent Superior Proposal; provided that any amendment to any material term or condition of any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal shall require a new notice and a new negotiation period that shall expire on the later to occur of (I) three (3) business days following delivery of such new notice from Parent to the Company and (II) the expiration of the original four (4) business day period, and (E) following the end of such negotiation period, the Board of Directors of Parent shall have determined in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, and after considering the terms of any proposed amendment or modification to this Agreement by the Company, that (x) the Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal that is the subject of the notice described in clause (B) above still constitutes a Parent Superior Proposal and (y) the failure to take such action would still be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law.

 

-57-

(d) In addition to the obligations of Parent set forth in Section 5.3(a) and Section 5.3(c), Parent shall promptly, and in any event within twenty-four (24) hours of receipt thereof, advise the Company in writing of any Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, or any inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer that would reasonably be expected to result in a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal, the identity of the person making such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer, the material terms thereof and copies of all portions of documents and substantive written communications received by Parent or any of its Representatives with respect thereto, in each case that contain terms and conditions thereto (or, as applicable, a summary of any substantive verbal communications received by Parent or any of its Representatives with respect thereto). Parent shall (i) keep the Company reasonably informed of the status and details of any such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer on a reasonably current basis, including any amendments or proposed amendments to the material terms thereof, and (ii) provide to the Company as soon as reasonably practicable after receipt or delivery thereof copies of all substantive written communications that contain terms and conditions thereto and all drafts of definitive documentation exchanged between Parent or its subsidiaries or any of their Representatives, on the one hand, and any person making such Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal or such inquiry, indication of interest, proposal or offer, on the other hand (or, as applicable, a summary of any substantive verbal communications with respect thereto).

(e) Other than in connection with a Parent Alternative Transaction Proposal (which shall be subject to Section 5.3(c)), prior to obtaining the Parent Stockholder Approvals, the Board of Directors of Parent may, in response to a Parent Intervening Event, make a Parent Recommendation Change pursuant to clause (i), clause (iii) or clause (v) of the definition thereof if, and only if, (A) the Board of Directors of Parent determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, that the failure to take such action would be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law, (B) Parent has notified the Company in writing that it intends to effect a Parent Recommendation Change pursuant to this Section 5.3(e), including the reasons therefor in reasonable detail, (C) for a period of four (4) business days following the notice delivered pursuant to clause (B) of this Section 5.3(e), Parent shall have discussed and negotiated in good faith and made Parent’s Representatives available to discuss and negotiate in good faith (in each case to the extent the Company desires to negotiate) with the Company’s Representatives any proposed modifications to the terms and conditions of this Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement so that the failure to effect a Parent Recommendation Change would no longer be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law; provided that any change to the material facts and circumstances relating to such Parent Intervening Event shall require a new notice and a new negotiation period that shall expire on the later to occur of (I) three (3) business days following delivery of such new notice from Parent to the Company and (II) the expiration of the original four (4) business day period, and (D) following the end of such negotiation period, the Board of Directors of Parent shall have determined in good faith, after consultation with its outside financial advisors and outside legal counsel, and after considering the terms of any proposed amendment or modification to this Agreement by the Company, that the failure to effect a Parent Recommendation Change would still be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties under Applicable Law.

(f) Nothing contained in this Section 5.3 shall prohibit or restrict Parent from (i) taking and disclosing to its stockholders a position contemplated by Rule 14d-9 or Rule 14e-2(a) promulgated under the Exchange Act, or from issuing a “stop, look and listen” statement or similar communication of the type contemplated by Rule 14d-9(f) under the Exchange Act pending disclosure of its position thereunder and (ii) making any required disclosure to the stockholders of Parent if the Board of Directors of Parent determines in good faith, after consultation with its outside counsel, that the failure to take such action would be reasonably likely to be inconsistent with Applicable Law; provided, that Parent expressly reaffirms the Parent Board Recommendation in any such public disclosure, other than such a “stop, look and listen” statement or similar communication; provided, further, that Parent shall not effect a Parent Recommendation Change other than pursuant to Section 5.3(c) or Section 5.3(e).

 

-58-

(g) Any action by (i) any controlled affiliate of Parent or any director or officer of Parent or any Parent Financial Advisor or (ii) any other Representative of Parent or any of its subsidiaries to the extent Parent authorizes, or knowingly permits such action that, in each case, if taken by Parent would be a breach of this Section 5.3, shall be deemed to constitute a breach by Parent of this Section 5.3; provided that any action by any Parent Financial Advisor shall not constitute a breach of this Section 5.3 by the Parent for the purposes of Section 7.3(b), Section 7.3(d) or Section 8.1(d) unless the Company authorizes, or knowingly permits, such action.

ARTICLE VI

ADDITIONAL AGREEMENTS

Section 6.1. Preparation of the Form S-4 and the Joint Proxy Statement; Stockholders Meetings.

(a) As soon as practicable following the date of this Agreement, the Company and Parent shall jointly prepare the Form S-4, in which the Joint Proxy Statement shall be included as a prospectus, and Parent shall file the Form S-4 with the SEC. Each of the Company and Parent shall use reasonable best efforts to have the Form S-4 declared effective under the Securities Act as promptly as practicable after such filing. The Company and Parent shall, as promptly as practicable after receipt thereof, provide the other party copies of any written comments and advise the other party of any oral comments, with respect to the Form S-4 or the Joint Proxy Statement received from the SEC. Parent and the Company shall cooperate and provide the other party with a reasonable opportunity to review and comment on any amendment or supplement to the Form S-4 or the Joint Proxy Statement prior to filing such with the SEC. Notwithstanding any other provision herein to the contrary, no amendment or supplement (including by incorporation by reference) to the Form S-4 or the Joint Proxy Statement shall be made without the approval of both the Company and Parent, which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed; provided that, with respect to documents filed by a party that are incorporated by reference in the Form S-4 or the Joint Proxy Statement, this right of approval shall apply only with respect to information relating to the other party or its business, financial condition or results of operations, or the combined entity; and provided, further, that this approval right shall not apply with respect to information relating to a Company Recommendation Change or a Parent Recommendation Change, as applicable, made in compliance with this Agreement. The Company shall use reasonable best efforts to cause the Joint Proxy Statement to be mailed to the Company’s stockholders, and Parent shall use reasonable best efforts to cause the Joint Proxy Statement to be mailed to Parent’s stockholders, in each case as promptly as practicable after the Form S-4 is declared effective under the Securities Act. Each party shall advise the other parties, promptly after it receives notice thereof, of the time when each of the Form S-4 has become effective, of the time when any supplement or amendment to the Form S-4 has been filed, of the issuance of any stop order with respect to the Form S-4, or of any request by the SEC for amendment of the Form S-4 or comments on the Form S-4 and responses thereto or requests by the SEC for additional information relating thereto. If at any time prior to the Effective Time any information relating to the Company, Parent or any of their respective affiliates, officers or directors, is discovered by the Company or Parent that should be set forth in an amendment or supplement to any of the Form S-4 or the Joint Proxy Statement so that any such document would not include any misstatement of a material fact or omit to state any material fact necessary to make the statements therein, in light of the circumstances under which they were made, not misleading, the party that discovers such information shall promptly notify the other party and an appropriate amendment or supplement describing such information shall be promptly filed with the SEC and, to the extent required by Applicable Law, disseminated to the stockholders of the Company and Parent.

 

-59-

(b) The Company shall, as promptly as practicable after the Form S-4 is declared effective under the Securities Act, duly give notice of, convene and hold a meeting of its stockholders (the “Company Stockholders Meeting”) in accordance with the DGCL and Nasdaq rules for the purpose of obtaining the Company Stockholder Approval and shall, subject to the provisions of Section 5.2(c) and Section 5.2(e), through its Board of Directors, recommend to its stockholders the adoption of this Agreement. Notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary (including any Company Recommendation Change), unless this Agreement is terminated in accordance with Article VIII, the Company shall hold the Company Stockholders Meeting and submit the adoption and approval of this Agreement and the Merger for approval by the stockholders of the Company and shall, without limiting Section 5.2, use reasonable best efforts, and cause its Representatives to use reasonable best efforts, to obtain from the stockholders of the Company the Company Stockholder Approval at the Company Stockholders Meeting. The Company may only postpone or adjourn the Company Stockholders Meeting (i) to solicit additional proxies if necessary for the purpose of obtaining the Company Stockholder Approval, (ii) for the absence of a quorum and (iii) to allow reasonable additional time for the filing and/or mailing of any supplemental or amended disclosure that the Company has determined after consultation with outside legal counsel is reasonably likely to be required under Applicable Law and for such supplemental or amended disclosure to be disseminated and reviewed by stockholders of the Company prior to the Company Stockholders Meeting; provided that the Company shall postpone or adjourn the Company Stockholders Meeting up to two (2) times for up to thirty (30) calendar days each time upon the reasonable request of Parent.

(c) Parent shall, as promptly as practicable after the Form S-4 is declared effective under the Securities Act, duly give notice of, convene and hold a meeting of its stockholders (the “Parent Stockholders Meeting”) in accordance with the DGCL and Nasdaq rules for the purpose of obtaining the Parent Stockholder Approvals and shall, subject to the provisions of Section 5.3(c) and Section 5.3(e), through its Board of Directors, recommend to its stockholders the approval of the Parent Charter Amendment and the Parent Share Issuance. Notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary (including any Parent Recommendation Change), unless this Agreement is terminated in accordance with Article VIII, Parent shall hold the Parent Stockholders Meeting and submit the Parent Charter Amendment and the Parent Share Issuance for approval by the stockholders of Parent and shall, without limiting Section 5.3, use reasonable best efforts, and cause its Representatives to use reasonable best efforts, to obtain from the stockholders of Parent the Parent Stockholder Approvals at the Parent Stockholders Meeting. Parent may only postpone or adjourn the Parent Stockholders Meeting (i) to solicit additional proxies if necessary for the purpose of obtaining the Parent Stockholder Approvals, (ii) for the absence of a quorum and (iii) to allow reasonable additional time for the filing and/or mailing of any supplemental or amended disclosure that Parent has determined after consultation with outside legal counsel is reasonably likely to be required under Applicable Law and for such supplemental or amended disclosure to be disseminated and reviewed by stockholders of Parent prior to the Parent Stockholders Meeting; provided, that Parent shall postpone or adjourn the Parent Stockholders Meeting up to two (2) times for up to thirty (30) calendar days each time upon the reasonable request of the Company.

(d) The Company and Parent shall use reasonable best efforts to hold the Company Stockholders Meeting and the Parent Stockholders Meeting on the same date and as soon as reasonably practicable after the date of this Agreement.

(e) The only matters to be voted upon at each of the Company Stockholders Meeting and the Parent Stockholders Meeting are (i) the adoption of this Agreement, in the case of the Company Stockholders Meeting, and the Parent Charter Amendment and the Parent Share Issuance, in the case of the Parent Stockholders Meeting, (ii) any adjournment or postponement of the Company Stockholders Meeting or the Parent Stockholders Meeting, as applicable, and (iii) any other matters that are required by Applicable Law.

 

-60-

(f) If a holder of shares of Company Series A Preferred Stock has made a timely and valid Preferred Stock Election in accordance with Section 3.1(a)(ii), Parent shall, prior to the Closing, file the Parent Series B Certificate of Designation with the Secretary of State of the State of Delaware.

Section 6.2. Access to Information; Confidentiality. Subject to the Confidentiality Agreement and subject to Applicable Law, and solely for the purposes of consummating the Merger and good faith integration planning, upon reasonable notice, each party shall, and shall cause its subsidiaries to, afford to the other party and to the officers, employees and Representatives of such other party, reasonable access, during normal business hours during the period from the date of this Agreement to the Effective Time, to all of its and their respective properties, books, Contracts, commitments, personnel and records (provided that such access shall not unreasonably interfere with the business or operations of such party), and during such period, each party shall, and shall cause its subsidiaries to, furnish promptly to the other party all information concerning its and their business, properties and personnel as such other party may reasonably request; provided, that the foregoing shall not require the Company, Parent or their respective subsidiaries to disclose any information pursuant to this Section 6.2 to the extent that (i) in the reasonable good faith judgment of such party, any Applicable Law requires such party or its subsidiaries to restrict or prohibit access to any such information, (ii) in the reasonable good faith judgment of such party, the information is subject to confidentiality obligations to a third party, (iii) disclosure of any such information or document would result in the loss of attorney-client privilege, attorney work product or other relevant legal privilege, or (iv) disclosure of any such information would result in the disclosure of any valuations of the Company or Parent in connection with the transactions contemplated hereby or portions of the minutes of the meetings of the Board of Directors of the Company or the Board of Directors of Parent or any committee thereof (including any presentations or other materials prepared by or for the Board of Directors of the Company or the Board of Directors of Parent or any committee thereof) where the Board of Directors of the Company or the Board of Directors of Parent or such committee, as applicable, discussed the transactions contemplated hereby or any similar transaction between the Company or Parent and any other person, or would result in the disclosure of any information that is reasonably pertinent to a litigation or other dispute where Parent or any of its affiliates, on the one hand, and the Company or any of its affiliates, on the other hand, are adverse parties; provided, further, that, with respect to the foregoing clauses (i) through (iii), the Company or Parent, as applicable, shall use its reasonable best efforts to (A) obtain the required consent of any third party necessary to provide such disclosure, (B) develop an alternative to providing such information so as to address such matters that is reasonably acceptable to the other party and (C) utilize the procedures of a joint defense agreement or implement such other techniques if the parties determine that doing so would reasonably permit the disclosure of such information without violating Applicable Law or jeopardizing such privilege. No review pursuant to this Section 6.2 shall affect any representation or warranty given by the other party hereto. Any information provided or made available pursuant to this Section 6.2 shall be governed by the terms and conditions of the Confidentiality Agreement.

Section 6.3. Reasonable Best Efforts.

(a) Subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement, Parent and the Company will cooperate with each other and use (and will cause their respective subsidiaries to use) their respective reasonable best efforts to consummate the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, and in any event prior to the Outside Date, including using reasonable best efforts to accomplish the following as promptly as reasonably practicable: (i) the obtaining of all actions or non-actions, consents, approvals, registrations, waivers, permits, authorizations, Orders, expirations or terminations of waiting periods and other confirmations from any Governmental Entity that are or may become necessary, proper or advisable in connection with the consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including the Merger, (ii) the preparation and making of all registrations, filings, forms, notices, petitions, statements, submissions of information, applications and other documents with Governmental Entities that are or may

 

-61-

become necessary, proper or advisable in connection with the consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including the Merger, (iii) the taking of all steps as may be necessary, proper or advisable to obtain an approval from, or to avoid an Action by, any Governmental Entity in connection with the consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including the Merger, and (iv) defending any lawsuits or other Actions, whether judicial or administrative, challenging this Agreement or that would otherwise prevent or delay the consummation of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, including the Merger, performed or consummated by each party in accordance with the terms of this Agreement, including seeking to have any stay, temporary restraining order or injunction entered by any court or other Governmental Entity vacated or reversed. Each of Parent and the Company shall, in consultation and cooperation with the other (A) as promptly as reasonably practicable, but in no event later than 15 business days from the date of this Agreement, make its respective filing under the HSR Act, and (B) as promptly as practicable, but in no event later than as required by Applicable Law, make any other filings under any Regulatory Law set forth in Section 7.1(c) of the Parent Disclosure Letter and any other applications and filings as reasonably determined by Parent or the Company (in good faith consultation with each other) under other applicable Regulatory Laws with respect to the transactions contemplated by this Agreement. Neither Parent nor the Company will (1) withdraw any such filings or applications or (2) agree to any extension of any waiting period or commit not to consummate the Merger for any period of time, in each case without the prior written consent of the other party (not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed).

(b) In connection with and without limiting the efforts referenced in Section 6.3(a), each of the parties shall consult and cooperate in all respects with one another, and consider in good faith the views of one another, in connection with the actions, communications, decisions and strategy for obtaining any consents, clearances, registrations, approvals, permits, waivers, expirations or terminations of waiting periods and authorizations, under the HSR Act and any other Regulatory Law; provided that Parent shall, after consulting with the Company and considering in good faith the views of the Company, have final decision-making authority with respect to such actions, communications, decisions and strategy.

(c) Each party shall (i) promptly notify the other parties of any substantive communication to that party from any Governmental Entity regarding this Agreement, the Merger or the other transactions contemplated hereby and, subject to Applicable Law, permit the other parties to review, reasonably in advance, any written communication or presentation proposed to be submitted to any Governmental Entity with respect to the foregoing and consider in good faith any comments such other party may provide thereto; (ii) not agree to participate in any substantive meeting or discussion with any Governmental Entity in respect of any filings, investigation or inquiry under any Regulatory Law or any other Applicable Law in connection with this Agreement or the Merger and the other transactions contemplated hereby unless in each case it consults with the other parties in advance and, to the extent reasonably practicable and permitted by such Governmental Entity, gives the other parties the opportunity to attend and participate thereat; and (iii) furnish the other parties with copies of all filings (other than Item 4(c) and Item 4(d) documents as defined in the HSR Act) on a “counsel only” basis and material correspondences and communications (and memoranda setting forth the substance thereof) between them and their respective affiliates and representatives, on the one hand, and any Governmental Entity or members or their respective staffs, on the other hand, with respect to any Regulatory Law or any other Applicable Law in connection with this Agreement. The parties hereto shall consult and cooperate with one another in connection with any analyses, appearances, presentations, memoranda, briefs, arguments, opinions and proposals made or submitted by or on behalf of any party hereto relating to proceedings under any Regulatory Law or any other Applicable Law in connection with this Agreement or the Merger and the other transactions contemplated hereby. Notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary, each of the parties hereto (and each of their respective affiliates) may, as each reasonably deems necessary or advisable: (x) designate any competitively sensitive material provided to the others under

 

-62-

this Section 6.3(c) as “outside counsel only” and such materials and the information contained therein shall be given only to the outside legal counsel of the recipient and will not be disclosed by such outside counsel to employees, officers or directors of the recipient unless express written consent is obtained in advance from the source of the materials; and (y) redact documents and information provided to the others under this Section 6.3(c) as necessary to comply with Applicable Law, or as necessary to avoid adversely impacting or jeopardizing any legal privilege; provided that the parties shall use reasonable best efforts to provide such documents or information in such alternative manner as would comply with Applicable Law and would not impact or jeopardize any legal privilege.

(d) In furtherance of and without limiting the efforts referenced in Section 6.3(a), Parent and the Company, along with their respective subsidiaries and affiliates, shall, to the extent necessary to avoid or eliminate each and every impediment under any Regulatory Law that may be asserted by any Governmental Entity so as to enable the consummation of the Merger as promptly as practicable, and in any event by the Outside Date: (A) agree to hold separate, sell, license, divest, restructure or otherwise dispose of any of the businesses or properties or assets of Parent, the Company or any of their respective subsidiaries, (B) terminate, amend or assign any contractual rights or obligations, joint venture or other legal partnership or relationship, (C) grant any right or commercial or other accommodation to (including a modification to existing terms), or enter into any contractual or other commercial relationship with or for the benefit of, any third party (including agreeing to minimum supply, pricing or other similar commitments to third parties) or (D) impose any restrictions, requirements or any behavioral, commercial or other limitations on Parent or any of its subsidiaries or the Company or any of its subsidiaries with respect to how they own, retain, conduct or operate all or any portion of their respective businesses or assets (clauses (A) through (D), a “Remedial Action”); provided that, notwithstanding anything in this Agreement to the contrary, neither Parent nor any of its subsidiaries shall be required to effect, agree to effect or propose to effect any Remedial Action that (1) would reasonably be expected to be material (in an adverse manner) to the business of Parent and the Company and their respective subsidiaries, taken as a whole, or (2) is not conditioned upon the consummation of the Merger (any such Remedial Action in clauses (1) or (2), a “Burdensome Condition”). The Company and its subsidiaries shall not effect, agree to effect or propose to effect any Remedial Action without the prior written consent of Parent. The Company and its applicable subsidiaries shall agree to effect any Remedial Action relating to the Company and its subsidiaries as directed by Parent; provided that any such action is conditioned upon the consummation of the Merger. Each of Parent and the Company shall not, and shall cause each of its subsidiaries and affiliates not to, acquire or agree to acquire any business or securities of any person that offers products or services competitive with the businesses of Parent or the Company, if such acquisitions would reasonably be expected to make it materially more likely that the expiration of any waiting period under the HSR Act or any approval from any Governmental Entity necessary to consummate the Merger pursuant to the terms of this Agreement does not occur, is not obtained or is materially delayed.

Section 6.4. Indemnification, Exculpation and Insurance.

(a) Parent agrees that all rights to indemnification and exculpation from liabilities, including advancement of expenses, for acts or omissions occurring at or prior to the Effective Time now existing in favor of the current or former directors, officers, employees or agents of the Company and its subsidiaries (the “D&O Indemnified Parties”) as provided in the Certificate of Incorporation of the Company, the Bylaws of the Company, the governing or organizational document of any subsidiary of the Company or any indemnification contract between such D&O Indemnified Parties and the Company or any of its subsidiaries (in each case, as in effect on or prior to the date of this Agreement and, in the case of any indemnification contracts, (i) to the extent made available to Parent prior to the date of this Agreement or (ii) that are between the Company or its subsidiaries on, the one hand, and any of their respective current or former directors or officers, on the other hand, and are substantially consistent with

 

-63-

the form of indemnification contract filed in the Company’s SEC Documents) shall survive the Merger and shall continue in full force and effect and shall cause the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, and its subsidiaries (including by providing sufficient funds) to honor and fulfill, in all respects, the obligations of the Company and its subsidiaries in respect of such rights to indemnification and exculpation. For a period of six (6) years from the Effective Time, the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, shall, and Parent shall cause the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, to, maintain in effect the exculpation, indemnification and advancement of expenses at least as favorable as the provisions of the Certificate of Incorporation of the Company, the Bylaws of the Company and the governing or organizational document of any subsidiary of the Company as in effect immediately prior to the Effective Time with respect to acts or omissions occurring prior to the Effective Time and shall not amend, repeal or otherwise modify any such provisions in any manner that would adversely affect the rights thereunder of any D&O Indemnified Parties; provided that all rights to indemnification in respect of any claim made (or notification to Parent that a D&O Indemnified Party intends to make a claim) for indemnification within such period shall continue until the disposition of such action or resolution of such claim. From and after the Effective Time, Parent shall guarantee and stand surety for, and shall cause the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, and its subsidiaries to honor, in accordance with their respective terms, each of the covenants contained in this Section 6.4.

(b) From and after the Effective Time, Parent and the Second Surviving Corporation shall jointly and severally indemnify and hold harmless each person who is or was a director or executive officer (for the purposes of this provision, “executive officer” includes those who, while serving as a director or executive officer of the Company, is or was serving at the request of the Company as a director, officer, employee, or agent of another corporation, including service with respect to an employee benefit plan) of the Company or any of its subsidiaries (each, together with such person’s heirs, successors, executors, administrators or personal or legal representatives, an “Indemnified Party”), against all obligations to pay any threatened, asserted, pending or completed judgment, damages, settlement, losses, liabilities, or fine or penalty, interest and reasonable expenses (including legal expenses and other reasonable expenses that may be incurred by any Indemnified Party in successfully enforcing the indemnity and other obligations provided in this Section 6.4) incurred in connection with any Action (such amounts, the “Claim Expenses”, and such claims made against any Indemnified Party, a “D&O Claim”), whether civil, criminal, administrative, arbitrative or investigative, and whether formal or informal, by reason of the fact that the Indemnified Party is or was an executive officer, director or employee of the Company or its subsidiaries, or an executive officer, director or employee of another entity or Company Benefit Plan if such service was at the request of the Company, whether asserted or claimed prior to, at, or after the Effective Time, to the fullest extent provided for under Applicable Law, the organizational documents of the Company and its subsidiaries and indemnification agreements of the Company and its subsidiaries in existence as of the date hereof. From and after the Effective Time, Parent shall advance to such Indemnified Party the Claim Expenses incurred by the Indemnified Party in connection with any D&O Claim prior to its final disposition, and such advancement shall be made as soon as reasonably practicable, but in any event no later than 30 days, after the receipt by Parent or the Surviving Corporation of a written statement or statements requesting such advances from time to time (which shall include invoices received by such Indemnified Party in connection with such Claim Expenses but, in the case of invoices in connection with legal services, to the extent any references to legal work performed or to expenditure made that would cause the Indemnified Party to waive any privilege accorded by Applicable Law, such references shall not be included with the invoice); provided that any such advances shall be unsecured and interest free and made without regard to the Indemnified Party’s ability to repay such advances; provided, further, any person to whom expenses are advanced provides an undertaking to repay such advances if it is ultimately determined that such person is not entitled to be indemnified by Parent or the Surviving Corporation; provided, further, that Parent or the Surviving Corporation shall not be obligated to make any advancement to the extent advancement is prohibited by Applicable Law, the organizational documents of the Company and its subsidiaries or any indemnification agreement in existence as of the date hereof to which such person and the Company or any of its subsidiaries is a party.

 

-64-

(c) Prior to the Effective Time, the Company shall use reasonable best efforts to, or, if the Company is unable to, Parent shall cause the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, as of or after the Effective Time to, purchase a six (6)-year prepaid “tail” policy, with terms, conditions, retentions and limits of liability that are no less favorable than the coverage provided under the Company’s existing policies of directors’ and officers’ liability insurance and fiduciary liability insurance, with respect to matters arising on or before the Effective Time, and Parent shall cause such policy to be maintained in full force and effect, for its full term, and cause all obligations thereunder to be honored by the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, and no other party shall have any further obligation to purchase or pay for insurance hereunder; provided that the Company shall not pay, and the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, shall not be required to pay, in excess of 300% of the last annual premium paid by the Company prior to the date of this Agreement in respect of such “tail” policy; provided, further, that if the cost of such “tail” policy exceeds such amount, the Company, the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as applicable, shall obtain a policy with the greatest comparable coverage available for a cost not exceeding such amount.

(d) The covenants contained in this Section 6.4 shall survive the Closing and are intended to be for the benefit of, and shall be enforceable by, each of the D&O Indemnified Parties and their respective heirs and shall not be deemed exclusive of any other rights to which any such person is entitled, whether pursuant to Applicable Law, Contract or otherwise.

(e) In the event that Parent, the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation or any of their respective successors or assigns (i) consolidates with or merges into any other person and shall not be the continuing or surviving corporation or entity of such consolidation or merger or (ii) transfers or conveys all or substantially all of its properties and assets to any person, then, and in each such case, proper provision shall be made so that the successors or assigns of Parent, the Surviving Corporation or the Second Surviving Corporation, as the case may be, shall assume the obligations set forth in this Section 6.4.

Section 6.5. Consents; No Consent Fee. Upon the terms and subject to the conditions set forth in this Agreement, Parent and its subsidiaries, on the one hand, and the Company and its subsidiaries, on the other hand, will use their respective reasonable best efforts to (a) seek to obtain all consents, waivers and approvals and (b) deliver all notifications, in each case pursuant to any Company Material Contracts in connection with this Agreement and the Merger so as to seek to maintain and preserve the benefits to the Surviving Corporation and the Second Surviving Corporation of such Company Material Contracts as of and following the consummation of the Merger; provided that without the prior written consent of Parent, neither the Company nor any of its respective subsidiaries or affiliates will grant or offer to grant any accommodation or concession (financial or otherwise), make or commit to make any payment or increased payment, or provide any additional security (including a guaranty) to any such third party in connection with seeking or obtaining its consent to the transactions contemplated by this Agreement.

Section 6.6. Fees and Expenses. Except as set forth in Section 8.2, all fees and expenses incurred in connection with the Merger, this Agreement and the transactions contemplated by this Agreement shall be paid by the party incurring such fees or expenses, whether or not the Merger is consummated.

Section 6.7. Public Announcements. The Company, Parent and the Merger Subs shall consult with each other before issuing any press release or making any public statement with respect to this Agreement or the Merger and shall not issue any such press release or make any such public statement without the prior consent of the other, such consent not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the first sentence of this Section 6.7 shall not apply with respect to (a) any such press release or public statement as may be required by Applicable Law or any listing agreement with any national securities exchange may be issued prior to such consultation if the party making the release or statement has used its reasonable best efforts to consult with the other party prior to such issuance, (b) a Company Recommendation Change (or any responses thereto), a Parent Recommendation Change (or any responses thereto) or in connection with a communication made pursuant to Section 5.2(f) or Section 5.3(f) (or any responses thereto), (c) any disclosure of information concerning this Agreement or the transactions contemplated hereby in connection with any dispute between the parties regarding this Agreement or the transactions contemplated hereby, (d) any such content that has been previously consented to by the other party, or otherwise exempted from this Section 6.7, to the extent replicated in whole or in part in any subsequent press release or other announcement, and (e) any public statement regarding the Merger in response to questions from the press, analysts, investors or those attending industry conferences, or to internal announcements to employees, so long as any such statement substantially reiterates (or is consistent with) previous press releases, public disclosures or public statements made jointly by the parties and otherwise in compliance with this Section 6.7 and do not reveal material nonpublic information regarding this Agreement or the transactions contemplated by this Agreement.

Section 6.8. Nasdaq Listing. Parent shall use reasonable best efforts to cause the Parent Common Stock issuable under Article III to be approved for listing on Nasdaq, subject to official notice of issuance, prior to the Closing Date. The Company will cooperate with Parent as reasonably requested by Parent with respect to the listing on Nasdaq and use reasonable best efforts to promptly furnish to Parent all information concerning itself and its members that may be required or reasonably requested in connection with any action contemplated by this Section 6.8.

Section 6.9. Certain Tax Matters.

(a) For U.S. federal income Tax purposes, Parent, the Merger Subs and the Company (i) intend that the First Merger and the Second Merger, taken together, constitute an integrated plan and shall qualify as a single “reorganization” within the meaning of Section 368(a) of the Code (the “Intended Tax Treatment”), and the Treasury Regulations promulgated thereunder, and (ii) hereby adopt this Agreement as a “plan of reorganization” within the meaning of Sections 354 and 361 of the Code. Each of Parent, the Merger Subs and the Company shall not, and shall not permit or cause any of its respective subsidiaries to, take any action that could, or agree to take any action that could, reasonably be expected to prevent or impede the Merger from qualifying for the Intended Tax Treatment. Each of Parent and the Company shall promptly notify the other party if, at any time before the Effective Time, Parent or the Company, as the case may be, becomes aware of the existence of any fact or circumstance that could reasonably be expected to prevent or impede the Merger from qualifying for the Intended Tax Treatment. Unless otherwise required by a change in Applicable Law after the date hereof or a determination within the meaning of Section 1313(a) of the Code, (x) the parties shall treat, for U.S. federal and applicable state and local income tax purposes, the Merger as a “reorganization” within the meaning of Section 368(a) of the Code, (y) no party shall take any position for Tax purposes inconsistent therewith and (z) the parties shall comply with all reporting and recordkeeping requirements applicable to the Merger that are prescribed by the Code, the Treasury Regulations and forms, instructions or other publications of the IRS, including the recordkeeping and information-filing requirements prescribed by Treasury Regulations Section 1.368-3. Notwithstanding any provision in this Agreement to the contrary, none of Parent, the Company or any subsidiary of either Parent or the Company shall have any liability or obligation to any holder of Company Capital Stock should the Merger fail to qualify as a “reorganization” within the meaning of Section 368(a) of the Code.

 

-66-

(b) The Company shall use reasonable best efforts to cause Davis Polk & Wardwell LLP (or such other nationally recognized counsel as may be reasonably acceptable to the Company and Parent), counsel to the Company (“Company’s Counsel”), to render any opinion as to the U.S. federal income tax treatment of the Merger required in connection with the Form S-4. Parent and the Company shall use reasonable best efforts to deliver to Company’s Counsel one or more customary representation letters with respect to the qualification of the Merger for the Intended Tax Treatment and customary related matters, in form and substance reasonably satisfactory to Company’s Counsel, at such time (or times) as Company’s Counsel shall reasonably request, which may include (i) the date of the declaration of effectiveness of the Form S-4 by the SEC, (ii) on such other date (or dates) as determined reasonably necessary by Company’s Counsel in connection with the preparation and filing of the Form S-4, (iii) at the Effective Time and (iv) on such other dates as determined reasonably necessary or appropriate by Company’s Counsel. Each of Parent and the Company shall also use its reasonable best efforts to provide such other information as reasonably requested by Company’s Counsel for purposes of rendering any opinion with respect to the qualification of the Merger for the Intended Tax Treatment and customary related matters.

(c) Prior to the Closing, the Company shall deliver to Parent (i) an original signed statement from the Company, that the Company is not, and has not been at any time during the applicable period specified in Section 897(c)(1)(A)(ii) of the Code, a “United States real property holding corporation”, as defined in Section 897(c)(2) of the Code, conforming to the requirements of Treasury Regulations Section 1.1445-2(c)(3) and 1.897-2(h) and reasonably satisfactory to Parent, and (ii) an original signed notice to be delivered to the IRS in accordance with the provisions of Treasury Regulations Section 1.897-2(h)(2), together with written authorization for Parent to deliver such notice to the IRS on behalf of the Company following the Closing, each dated as of the Closing Date, duly executed by an authorized officer of the Company, and in form and substance reasonably satisfactory to Parent.

Section 6.10. Takeover Statutes. If any antitakeover or similar statute or regulation is or may become or purports to be applicable to the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, each of the parties hereto and its respective Board of Directors shall, to the extent permissible under Applicable Law, (a) grant such approvals and take all such actions as are legally permissible so that the transactions contemplated hereby may be consummated as promptly as practicable on the terms contemplated hereby and (b) otherwise act to eliminate or minimize the effects of any such statute or regulation on the transactions contemplated hereby.
